The fixed-three-row native recipe builds and passes all 104 catalogue groups, including nine added fixed-row equality, cropping and fallback assertions. The unchanged real-model lifecycle gate passes all 86 assertions under the existing ten-GB physical watchdog; its sampled physical peak is 7,971,526,944 bytes and duration is 96.645141 seconds. Reference attention, ordinary matmul and draft fusion remain exact. Prefix arithmetic identity is separately versioned. No old tolerance or assertion is loosened.

The subsequently frozen single fourteen-GB full-response pilot passes every physical, natural-completion and timing eligibility condition. It compares with all three already completed original controls, without repeating them or selecting favorable outputs. The complete coding text is identical. Generation is 12.885401 / 15.496890 / 14.562017 committed tokens/s for short/context/coding, versus original medians 12.752474 / 15.375956 / 14.968441. Candidate/original ratios are 1.010424 / 1.007865 / 0.972848. Full-request ratios are 0.996379 / 0.944842 / 1.047671. Neither the all-workload five-percent improvement nor the twenty-token alternative succeeds. This is a clean directional screen, not a statistical claim that the recipes are equal.

Reject this exact native recipe under its frozen stopping rule: no paired confirmation, task-quality expansion, production Auto admission or supported-default change. Its isolated projection improvement does not translate into useful complete-response improvement. Revert the experimental source while preserving its exact source patch, frozen build identity, complete lifecycle and timing data. The original remains preferred. This result does not prove all possible fixed-row, kernel or quantization techniques impossible; a new mechanism must earn its own cheap screen and a plausible role in the remaining low-budget gap before another implementation.
